\mathop{}\mkern-2.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.27777pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.27777pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.56596pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.27084pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-2.0mu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\hskip-2.0ptW_{k}\otimes W_{k}=e_{k}\otimes e_{k},

and for any
1\leq p\leq\infty
:

{\left\|W_{k}\otimes W_{k^{\prime}}\right\|}_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\lesssim\mu^{d-1-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle p$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle p$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$p$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$p$}}$}}}\qquad k\neq k^{\prime}.

This is
[
3 (C-L)
, Theorem 4.3]
. Claim (3) can be found proven there. The other two claims are straightforwardly deduced from the above definitions and estimates.

Corollary 4.1.1
(name=Mikado flows and fractional Sobolev norms).

By interpolation, claim (2) above implies its fractional version:

{\left\|W_{k}\right\|}_{W^{s,p}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\lesssim\mu^{s+\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle p$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle p$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$p$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$p$}}$}}}\qquad{\left\|{}_{k}\right\|}_{W^{s,p}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\lesssim\mu^{s-1+\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle p$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle p$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$p$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$p$}}$}}}.

4.2
Implementation of temporal concentration

Since Mikado flows are stationary, the velocity perturbation will be homogeneous in time if we simply use Decomposition
(??)
to define the coefficients. To obtain
L^{p}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}\cap L^{s}_{t}W^{\gamma,q}_{x}
estimates, it is necessary to introduce temporal concentration in the perturbation.

To this end, we choose temporal functions
g_{\kappa},h_{\kappa}
to oscillate the building blocks
W_{k}
intermittently in time. Specifically,
g_{\kappa}
will be used to oscillate
W_{k}
so that the space-time cascade balances the low temporal frequency part of the old stress error
\overline{R}
, whereas
h_{\kappa}
is used to define a temporal corrector whose time derivative will further balance the high temporal frequency part of the old stress error
\overline{R}
.

Let
g\!\in\!\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}([0,1])
be such that:

\int\limits_{0\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu1}g^{2}(t)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mut=1.

To add in temporal concentration, let
\kappa>0
be a large constant whose value will be specified later and define
g_{\kappa}:[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}
as the 1-periodic extension of
\kappa^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}g(\kappa t)
so that:

{\left\|g_{\kappa}\right\|}_{L^{p}([0,1])}\lesssim\kappa^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle p$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle p$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$p$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$p$}}$}}}\qquad\forall p\!\in\![1,\infty].

The value of
\kappa
will be specified later and the function
g_{\kappa}
will be used in the definition of the velocity perturbation. As we will see in
(??)
, the nonlinear term can only balance a portion of the stress
\overline{R}
and there is a leftover term which is of high temporal frequency. This motivates us to consider the following temporal corrector.

Let
h_{\kappa}:[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}
be defined by:

h_{\kappa}(t)=-t+\int\limits_{0\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mut}g_{\kappa}^{2}(s)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mus.

For
\kappa\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
, in view of the zero-mean condition for
g_{\kappa}^{2}(t)-1
, the function
h_{\kappa}:[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}
is well-defined and periodic, and we have:

{\left\|h_{\kappa}\right\|}_{L^{\infty}([0,1])}\leq 1,

uniformly in
\kappa
.

We remark that for any
\nu\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
, the periodically rescaled function
g_{\kappa}(\nu\cdot):[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}
also verifies the bound:

{\left\|g_{\kappa}(\nu\cdot)\right\|}_{L^{p}([0,1])}\lesssim\kappa^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle p$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle p$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$p$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$p$}}$}}}\qquad\forall p\!\in\![1,\infty].

Moreover, we have the identity:

\partial_{t}(\nu^{-1}h_{\kappa}(\nu t)\!)=g_{\kappa}^{2}-1,

which will imply the smallness of the corrector, cfr. the definition of
w^{(t)}
below.

4.3
Space-time cutoffs

Before introducing the velocity perturbation, we need to define two important cutoff functions, one to ensure
(??)
applies and the other to ensure the well-preparedness of the new solution
(u_{1},R_{1})
.

Since
(??)
is stated for a fixed compact set in
\mathcal{S}^{d\times d}_{+}
, we need to introduce a cutoff for the stress
\overline{R}
. Let
\chi:\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
be a positive smooth function such that
\chi
is monotonically increasing with respect to
|x|
and:

\chi(x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{\,}cl@{}}1&0\leq|x|\leq 1\\
|x|&|x|\geq 2\end{array}\right..

With this cutoff
\chi
, we may define a divisor for the stress
\overline{R}
so that
(??)
applies. Indeed, define
\rho\!\in\!\mathcal{C}^{\infty}([0,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{d})
by:

\rho=2\chi(\overline{R}).

Then immediately:

\operatorname{Id}-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\overline{R}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\rho$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\overline{R}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\rho$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\overline{R}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\rho$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\overline{R}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\rho$}}$}}\!\in\!B_{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}(\operatorname{Id})\qquad\forall(t,x)\!\in\![0,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{d},

which means we can use
\operatorname{Id}-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\overline{R}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\rho$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\overline{R}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\rho$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\overline{R}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\rho$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\overline{R}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\rho$}}$}}
as the argument in the smooth functions
\Gammaup_{k}
given by
(??)
.

Next, we need another cutoff to take care of the well-preparedness of the new solution
(u_{1},R_{1})
as the perturbation has to be supported within
I
. Let
\theta\!\in\!\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
be a smooth temporal cutoff function such that:

\theta(t)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{\,}cl@{}}1&\operatorname{dist}(t,I^{C})\geq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\tau\\
0&\operatorname{dist}(t,I^{C})\leq\tau\end{array}\right.,

where
I\!\subset\![0,1]
and
\tau>0
are the parameters of the well-preparedness of the glued solution
(\underline{u},\underline{R})
, thus required to satisfy
I\!\subseteq\!\tilde{I},0,1\!\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.33334pt\kern-4.99998pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\in}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.33334pt\kern-4.99998pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\in}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.69444pt\kern-4.18054pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\in}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.36115pt\kern-3.68057pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\in}}}\!I,\tau<2^{-1}\tilde{\tau}
, where
\tilde{I},\tilde{\tau}
are the parameters of the well-preparedness of the unglued solution
(u,R)
. Note that this cutoff ensures that the new solution will still be well-prepared.

4.4
The velocity perturbation

We recall the four parameters for the perturbation we have defined so far, and add a fourth one:

(1)

Temporal oscillation
\nu\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
;

(2)

Temporal concentration
\kappa>0
;

(3)

Spatial concentration
\mu\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
;

(4)

Spatial oscillation
\sigma\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
, which we introduce here, serves the purpose of making the
W_{k}
oscillate faster.

The requirements
\nu,\sigma,\mu\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
are for the sake of periodicity. We will now define and estimate the new Reynolds and the velocity perturbation in terms of these parameters, and obtain a series of relations that yield the bounds we desire, and then we will see for what ranges of
s,p,q,\gamma,\theta
we can choose the parameters so that the relations are satisfied.

With all the ingredients in hand, we are ready to define the velocity perturbation. In summary, the velocity perturbation
w:[0,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
consists of three parts:

w=w^{(p)}+w^{(c)}+w^{(t)}.

The principal part of the perturbation
w^{(p)}
consists of super-positions of the building blocks
W_{k}
oscillating with period
\sigma^{-1}
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and period
\nu^{-1}
on
[0,1]
:

w^{(p)}(t,x)\coloneq\sum_{k\in\Lambdaup}a_{k}(t,x)W_{l}(\sigma x),

where the amplitude function
a_{k}:[0,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
is given by:

a_{k}\coloneq\theta g_{\kappa}(\nu t)\rho^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\Gammaup_{k}{\left(\operatorname{Id}-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\overline{R}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\rho$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\overline{R}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\rho$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\overline{R}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\rho$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\overline{R}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\rho$}}$}}\right)}.

Note that the above
w^{(p)}
is not divergence-free. To fix this, we introduce a divergence-free corrector using the tensor potential
k
:

w^{(c)}(t,x)\coloneq\sigma^{-1}\cdot\!\sum_{k\in\Lambdaup}\nabla a_{k}(t,x)\mathbin{:}{}_{k}(\sigma x).

Indeed, we can rewrite:

\displaystyle w^{(p)}+w^{(c)}={}

\displaystyle\sigma^{-1}\cdot\!\sum a_{k}(t,x)\operatorname{div}{}_{k}(\sigma x)+\sigma^{-1}\cdot\!\sum\nabla a_{k}(t,x)\mathbin{:}{}_{k}(\sigma x)={}

\displaystyle{}={}

\displaystyle\sigma^{-1}\operatorname{div}\sum a_{k}(t,x){}_{k}(\sigma x),

where each
a_{k}{}_{k}
is skew-symmetric and hence double-divergence-free, making it so that
\operatorname{div}(w^{(p)}+w^{(c)})=0
.

Finally, we define a temporal corrector to balance the high temporal frequency part of the interaction. This Ansatz was first introduced in
[
2 (B-V)
]
and also used in
[
1 (B-C-V)
]
. The heart of the argument is to ensure that:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}w^{(t)}+\operatorname{div}(w^{(p)}\otimes w^{(p)})={}

Pressure gradient

\displaystyle{}+{}\text{Terms with high spacial frequencies}

\displaystyle{}+{}\text{Lower order terms}.

However, the key difference between
[
2 (B-V)
,
1 (B-C-V)
]
and the current scheme is that here the smallness of the corrector is free and it does not require much temporal oscillation, which is the reason we must use stationary spatial building blocks.

Specifically, the temporal corrector
w^{(t)}
is defined as:

w^{(t)}\coloneq\nu^{-1}h_{\kappa}(\nu t)(\operatorname{div}(\overline{R})-\nabla\Deltaup^{-1}\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}(\overline{R})\!),

where we note that
\Deltaup^{-1}
is well-defined on
\mathbb{T}^{d}
since
\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}(\overline{R})=\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\overline{R}_{ij}
has zero spatial mean.

It is easy to check
\operatorname{supp}_{t}w^{(t)}\!\subset\!\operatorname{supp}_{t}\overline{R}
and
w^{(t)}
is divergence-free. Indeed:

\operatorname{div}w^{(t)}=\nu^{-1}h_{\kappa}(\nu t)(\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\overline{R}_{ij}-\partial_{k}\partial_{k}\Deltaup^{-1}\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\overline{R}_{ij})=0.

In the lemma below, we show that the leading order interaction of the principal part
w^{(p)}
is able to balance the low temporal frequency part of the stress error
\overline{R}
, which motivates the choice of the corrector
w^{(t)}
.

Lemma 4.4.1
(name=Properties of the coefficients,label=thm:lemma:Propak).

The coefficients
a_{k}
satisfy:

a_{k}=0\quad\text{if}\quad\operatorname{dist}(t,I^{C})\leq\tau,

and:

\sum_{k\in\Lambdaup}a_{k}^{2}\mathop{}\mkern-2.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.27777pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.27777pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.56596pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.27084pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-2.0mu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}W_{k}\otimes W_{k}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux=\theta^{2}g_{\kappa}^{2}(\nu t)\rho\operatorname{Id}-g_{\kappa}^{2}(\nu t)\overline{R}.

The proof of this can be found in
[
3 (C-L)
]
, where it is Lemma 4.4.

4.5
The new Reynolds stress

In this subsection, our goal is to design a suitable stress tensor
R_{1}:[0,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{d}\to\mathcal{S}_{0}^{d\times d}
such that the pair
(u_{1},R_{1})
is a smooth solution of (
2
) for a suitable smooth pressure
p_{1}
.

We first compute the nonlinear term and isolate nonlocal interactions:

\operatorname{div}(w^{(p)}\otimes w^{(p)}+\overline{R})=\operatorname{div}{\left[\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}W_{k}(\sigma x)\otimes W_{k}(\sigma x)\right]}+\operatorname{div}R_{far},

where
R_{far}
denotes the nonlocal interactions between Mikado flows of different directions:

R_{far}=\sum_{k\neq k^{\prime}}a_{k}a_{k^{\prime}}W_{k}(\sigma x)\otimes W_{k^{\prime}}(\sigma x).

And then we proceed to examine the first term in the above decomposition, for which by
(??)
we have:

\operatorname{div}{\left[\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}W_{k}(\sigma x)\otimes W_{k}(\sigma x)\right]}={}\\
{}=\operatorname{div}{\left[\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}{\left[{\left(W_{k}(\sigma x)\otimes W_{k}(\sigma x)-\mathop{}\mkern-2.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.27777pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.27777pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.56596pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.27084pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-2.0mu\int W_{k}\otimes W_{k}\right)}+\mathop{}\mkern-2.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.27777pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.27777pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.56596pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.27084pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-2.0mu\int W_{k}\otimes W_{k}\right]}+\overline{R}\right]}={}\\
{}=\operatorname{div}\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}{\left(W_{k}(\sigma x)\otimes W_{k}(\sigma x)-\mathop{}\mkern-2.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.27777pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.27777pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.56596pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.27084pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-2.0mu\int W_{k}\otimes W_{k}\right)}+\nabla(\theta^{2}g_{\kappa}^{2}\rho)+(1-g_{\kappa}^{2}(\nu t)\!)\operatorname{div}\overline{R}.

Finally, using the product rule, we compute the divergence term as:

\operatorname{div}\sum_{k}a_{k}^{2}{\left(W_{k}(\sigma x)\otimes W_{k}(\sigma x)-\mathop{}\mkern-2.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.27777pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.27777pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.56596pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.27084pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-2.0mu\int W_{k}\otimes W_{k}\right)}=\sum_{k}\nabla(a_{k}^{2})\cdot{\left(W_{k}(\sigma x)\otimes W_{k}(\sigma x)-\mathop{}\mkern-2.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.27777pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.27777pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.56596pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.27084pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-2.0mu\int W_{k}\otimes W_{k}\right)}.

Typical in the convex integration, we can gain a factor of
\sigma^{-1}
in the above equality by inverting the divergence. To this end, let us use the bilinear anti-divergence operator
\mathcal{B}
from
(??)
. Since the above equality has zero spatial mean, by (
B.4
) it is equal to
\operatorname{div}R_{osc,x}
where:

R_{osc,x}=\sum_{k}\mathcal{B}{\left(\nabla(a_{k}^{2}),W_{k}(\sigma x)\otimes W_{k}(\sigma x)-\mathop{}\mkern-2.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.27777pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.27777pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.56596pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.27084pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-2.0mu\int W_{k}\otimes W_{k}\right)}.

Combining all the above, we have:

\operatorname{div}(w^{(p)}\otimes w^{(p)}+\overline{R})=\operatorname{div}R_{osc,x}+\operatorname{div}R_{far}+\nabla(\theta^{2}g_{\kappa}^{2}\rho)+(1-g_{\kappa}^{2}(\nu t)\!)\operatorname{div}\overline{R}.

In view of the above computations, we define a temporal oscillation error:

R_{osc,t}\coloneq\nu^{-1}h_{\kappa}(\nu t)\mathcal{R}\operatorname{div}(\partial_{t}\overline{R}),

so that the following decomposition holds.

Lemma 4.5.1
(name=Oscillation Reynolds and pressure,label=thm:lemma:OscReynPress).

Let the space-time oscillation error
R_{osc}
be:

R_{osc}\coloneq R_{osc,x}+R_{osc,t}+R_{far}.

Then:

\partial_{t}w^{(t)}+\operatorname{div}(w^{(p)}\otimes w^{(p)}+\overline{R})+\nabla P=\operatorname{div}R_{osc},

where the pressure term
P
is defined by:

P\coloneq\theta^{2}g_{\kappa}^{2}(\nu t)\rho-\nu^{-1}\Deltaup^{-1}\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}\partial_{t}(\overline{R}h_{\kappa}(\nu t)\!).

The proof of this is a simple calculation which is left to
[
3 (C-L)
]
, where it is Lemma 4.5.

Finally, we can define the correction error and the linear error as usual:

\displaystyle R_{cor}\coloneq{}

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(\operatorname{div}(w^{(c)}+w^{(t)})\otimes w+w^{(p)}\otimes(w^{(c)}+w^{(t)})\!)

\displaystyle R_{lin}\coloneq{}

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(\partial_{t}(w^{(p)}+w^{(c)})-\Deltaup\overline{u}+\overline{u}\otimes w+w\otimes\overline{u}).

To conclude, we summarize the main results in this section below.

Lemma 4.5.2
(name=The new Reynolds stress,label=thm:lemma:NewReyn).

Define the new Reynolds stress and pressure by:

\displaystyle R_{1}\coloneq{}

\displaystyle R_{lin}+R_{cor}+R_{osc}

\displaystyle p_{1}\coloneq{}

\displaystyle\overline{p}-P.

Then
(u_{1},R_{1})
is a well-prepared solution to NSR and the velocity perturbation
w\coloneq u_{1}-\overline{u}
satisfies
\operatorname{supp}w\!\subset\!I\times\mathbb{T}^{d}
.

The proof of this is also left to
[
3 (C-L)
]
, where it is Lemma 4.6.

5
Proof of the iteration proposition

In this section we will show that the velocity perturbation
w
and the new Reynolds stress
R_{1}
derived in the previous section satisfy the properties claimed in
(??)
.

As a general note, we use a constant
C_{u}
for dependency on the previous solution
(\overline{u},\overline{R})
throughout this section.

We now work out what relations between
\mu,\sigma,\nu,\kappa
we need for our estimates to hold, and then we see how we can achieve them.

5.1
Estimates on the velocity perturbation

We first estimate the coefficients
a_{k}
of the perturbation
w
. The following is
[
3 (C-L)
, Lemma 5.2]
.

Lemma 5.1
(name=Estimates on the perturbation coefficients,label=thm:lemma:Estak).

The coefficients
a_{k}
are smooth on
[0,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{d}
and:

{\left\|\partial_{t}^{n}\nabla^{m}a_{k}\right\|}_{L^{p}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C_{u,m,n}(\nu\kappa)^{n}\kappa^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle p$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle p$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$p$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$p$}}$}}}\qquad p\!\in\![1,\infty].

In addition, the following bound holds for all times
t\!\in\![0,1]
:

{\left\|a_{k}(t)\right\|}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\lesssim\theta(t)g_{\kappa}(\nu t){\left(\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{d}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt\rho(t,x)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\right)}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}.

With these estimates of
a_{k}
in hand, we start estimating the velocity perturbation. As expected, the principal part
w^{(p)}
is the largest among all parts in
w
. The following adapts
[
3 (C-L)
, Proposition 5.3]
.

Proposition 5.1
(name=Estimates on the principal part,label=thm:propo:Estwp).

Assume that

\displaystyle\sigma\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\nu^{2}

(5.1)

\displaystyle\kappa^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle p$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle p$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$p$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$p$}}$}}}\mu^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lesssim\lambda^{-\eta}

(5.2)

\displaystyle\sigma\kappa^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle s$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle s$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$s$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$s$}}$}}}\mu^{\gamma+\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle q$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle q$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$q$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$q$}}$}}}\lesssim\lambda^{-\eta}.

(5.3)

The principal part
w^{(p)}
satisfies:

{\left\|w^{(p)}\right\|}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim{\left\|\overline{R}\right\|}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}_{x}}+C_{u}\sigma^{-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\qquad{\left\|w^{(p)}\right\|}_{L^{p}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+{\left\|w^{(p)}\right\|}_{L^{s}_{t}W^{1,q}}\leq C_{u}\lambda^{-\eta}.

In particular, for sufficiently large
\lambda,\sigma
:

{\left\|w^{(p)}\right\|}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim{\left\|\overline{R}\right\|}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}_{x}}\qquad{\left\|w^{(p)}\right\|}_{L^{p}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+{\left\|w^{(p)}\right\|}_{L^{s}_{t}W^{1,q}_{x}}\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 4$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\delta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 4$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}.

Proof. (Sketch)

(
L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}
estimate)

The calculations in
[
3 (C-L)
]
lead to the following estimate:

{\left\|w^{(p)}\right\|}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim{\left\|\overline{R}\right\|}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}_{x}}+C_{u}\nu^{-1}+C_{u}\sigma^{-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}.

The desired estimate thus follows from
\nu^{-1}\lesssim\sigma^{-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}
, which is equivalent to (
5.1
).

(
L^{p}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}
estimate)

The calculations in
[
3 (C-L)
]
lead to the following bound:

{\left\|w^{(p)}\right\|}_{L^{p}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim_{u}\kappa^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle p$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle p$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$p$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$p$}}$}}}\mu^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}.

The desired estimate thus follows from (
5.2
).

(
L^{s}_{t}W^{\gamma,q}_{x}
estimate)

We take the
W^{\gamma,q}_{x}
norm to obtain:

{\left\|w^{(p)}(t)\right\|}_{W^{\gamma,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\lesssim\sum{\left\|a_{k}(t)\right\|}_{\mathcal{C}}{\left\|W_{k}(\sigma\cdot)\right\|}_{W^{\gamma,q}}.

Taking the
L^{s}
norm in time, by
(??)
and
(??)
we have that:

\displaystyle{\left\|w^{(p)}\right\|}_{L^{s}_{t}W^{\gamma,q}_{x}}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\sum_{k}{\left\|a_{k}\right\|}_{L^{s}_{t}\mathcal{C}_{x}}{\left\|W_{k}(\sigma\cdot)\right\|}_{W^{\gamma,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\lesssim\sigma\mu^{\gamma+\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle q$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle q$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$q$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$q$}}$}}}\cdot\mskip-4.0mu\sum_{k}{\left\|a_{k}\right\|}_{L^{s}_{t}\mathcal{C}_{x}}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle{}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\sigma\kappa^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle s$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle s$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$s$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$s$}}$}}}\mu^{\gamma+\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle q$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle q$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$q$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$q$}}$}}}.

The desired bound then follows from (
5.4
).

\Diamondx

Next, we estimate the corrector

.

Proposition 5.2

\displaystyle\lambda^{-\eta},

(5.6)

The divergence-free corrector
w^{(c)}
then satisfies:

{\left\|w^{(c)}\right\|}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2-r$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2r$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2-r$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2-r$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2-r$}}$}}}_{x}}\leq C_{u}\lambda^{-\eta}\qquad\qquad{\left\|w^{(c)}\right\|}_{L^{p}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+{\left\|w^{(c)}\right\|}_{L^{s}_{t}W^{\gamma,q}_{x}}\leq C_{u}\lambda^{-\eta}.

In particular, for sufficiently large
\lambda
:

{\left\|w^{(c)}\right\|}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2-r$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2r$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2-r$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2-r$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2-r$}}$}}}_{x}}\leq{\left\|\overline{R}\right\|}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}_{x}}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\qquad\qquad{\left\|w^{(c)}\right\|}_{L^{p}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+{\left\|w^{(c)}\right\|}_{L^{s}_{t}W^{\gamma,q}_{x}}\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 4$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\delta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 4$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}.

Remark 5.1
.

An estimate in
L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}
was proved at this point in
[
3 (C-L)
]
. However, that required a relation which severely limited the bounds on
s,q
, in such a way that they turned out decreasing in the dimension. The theorem, however, does not require such an estimate, and in fact the same estimate is not proved for
w^{(p)}
. Since one of the estimates for the Reynolds stress requires
L^{2}_{t}L^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2-r$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2r$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2-r$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2-r$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2-r$}}$}}}_{x}
, I will prove
L^{p}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}
and
L^{2}_{t}L^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2-r$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2r$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2-r$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2-r$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2-r$}}$}}}_{x}
.

Proof.

Since the first steps are the same for any exponent pair, we will do them in general, and then deduce the particular cases
L^{p}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}
and
L^{2}_{t}L^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2-r$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2r$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2-r$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2-r$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2-r$}}$}}}_{x}
.

(
L^{a}_{t}L^{b}_{x}
estimate)

From the definition, we have:

{\left\|w^{(c)}(t)\right\|}_{L^{b}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\leq\sigma^{-1}{\left\|\sum_{k}\nabla a_{k}(t)\mathbin{:}{}_{k}(\sigma\cdot)\right\|}_{L^{b}}\lesssim\sigma^{-1}\cdot\!\sum_{k}{\left\|\nabla a_{k}(t)\right\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}{\left\|{}_{k}(\sigma\cdot)\right\|}_{L^{b}_{x}}.

Now, thanks to
(??)
, we take
L^{2}_{t}
to obtain:

{\left\|w^{(c)}\right\|}_{L^{a}_{t}L^{b}_{x}}\lesssim\sigma^{-1}\mu^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}-1-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle b$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle b$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$b$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$b$}}$}}}\cdot\mskip-4.0mu\sum_{k}{\left\|\nabla a_{k}\right\|}_{L^{a}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C_{u}\sigma^{-1}\mu^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}-1-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle b$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle b$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$b$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$b$}}$}}}\kappa^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle a$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle a$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$a$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$a$}}$}}}.

The desired estimates thus follow from (
5.4
) and (
5.5
), the latter of which is obviously satisfied for
\eta
sufficiently small.

(
L^{s}_{t}W^{\gamma,q}_{x}
estimate)

This part is very similar to the estimation of
w^{(p)}
. We first take
W^{\gamma,q}_{x}
to obtain that:

\displaystyle{\left\|w^{(c)}(t)\right\|}_{W^{\gamma,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\leq{}

\displaystyle\sigma^{-1}{\left\|\sum_{k}\nabla a_{k}(t)\mathbin{:}{}_{k}(\sigma\cdot)\right\|}_{W^{\gamma,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}

\displaystyle{}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\sigma^{-1}\cdot\sum_{k}{\left\|a_{k}(t)\right\|}_{\mathcal{C}^{1+\gamma}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}{\left\|{}_{k}(\sigma\cdot)\right\|}_{W^{\gamma,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}.

Taking
L^{s}
norm in time and using
(??)
and
(??)
we have:

{\left\|w^{(c)}\right\|}_{L^{s}_{t}W^{\gamma,q}_{x}}\lesssim\sigma^{-1}\cdot\mskip-4.0mu\sum_{k}{\left\|a_{k}(t)\right\|}_{L^{s}_{t}\mathcal{C}^{2}_{x}}{\left\|{}_{k}(\sigma\cdot)\right\|}_{W^{\gamma,q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\lesssim\sigma^{\gamma-1}\kappa^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle s$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle s$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$s$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$s$}}$}}}\mu^{\gamma-1+\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle q$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle q$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$q$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$q$}}$}}}.

Once we require (
5.6
), this implies the desired bound.

\Diamondx
Finally, we estimate the temporal corrector
w^{(t)}
. From its definition, one can see that the spatial frequency of
w^{(t)}
is independent from the parameters
\sigma,\tau,\mu
. As a result, this term poses no constraints to the choice of temporal and spatial oscillation/concentration at all and is small for basically any choice of parameters (as long as temporal oscillation
\nu
is present). This is one of the main technical differences from
[
2 (B-V)
]
and
[
1 (B-C-V)
]
, where the leading order effect is temporal oscillation.

Proposition 5.3
(name=Estimates on the temporal corrector,label=thm:propo:Estwt).

The temporal corrector
w^{(t)}
satisfies:

{\left\|w^{(t)}\right\|}_{L^{\infty}_{t}W^{1,\infty}_{x}}\leq C_{u}\nu^{-1}.

In particular, for sufficiently large
\nu
:

{\left\|w^{(t)}\right\|}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\leq{\left\|\overline{R}\right\|}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}_{x}}\qquad{\left\|w^{(t)}\right\|}_{L^{p}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+{\left\|w^{(t)}\right\|}_{L^{s}_{t}W^{\gamma,q}_{x}}\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 4$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\delta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 4$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}.

Proof.

It follows directly from the definition of
w^{(t)}
that:

{\left\|w^{(t)}\right\|}_{L^{\infty}_{t}W^{\gamma,\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\nu^{-1}{\left\|h\right\|}_{L^{\infty}_{t}}{\left\|\overline{R}\right\|}_{L^{\infty}_{t}W^{\gamma+1,\infty}_{x}}\leq C_{u}\nu^{-1}.

5.2
Estimates on the new Reynolds stress

The last step of the proof is to estimate
R_{1}
. We proceed with the decomposition in
(??)
. More specifically, we will prove that, for all sufficiently large
\lambda
, each part of the stress
R_{1}
is less than
\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 4$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\delta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 4$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}
.

(
Linear error
)

Lemma 5.1
(name=Estimate on the linear error,label=thm:lemma:EstRlin).

Assume that:

\displaystyle 2\theta-1\leq{}

\displaystyle\gamma

(5.7)

\displaystyle r\leq{}

\displaystyle q,

(5.8)

\displaystyle\nu\kappa^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\sigma^{-1}\mu^{-1+\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle r$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle r$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$r$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$r$}}$}}}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\lambda^{-\eta}

(5.9)

\displaystyle\nu^{-1}\lesssim\lambda^{-\eta}.

(5.10)

Then, for sufficiently large
\lambda
,

{\left\|R_{lin}\right\|}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{r}_{x}}\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 4$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\delta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 4$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}.

Proof.

We split the linear error into three parts:

{\left\|R_{lin}\right\|}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{r}_{x}}\leq\underbrace{{\left\|\mathcal{R}((-\Deltaup)^{\theta}w)\right\|}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{r}_{x}}}_{\eqcolon L_{1}}+\underbrace{{\left\|\mathcal{R}(\partial_{t}(w^{(p)}+w^{(c)})\!)\right\|}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{r}_{x}}}_{\eqcolon L_{2}}+\underbrace{{\left\|\mathcal{R}(\operatorname{div}(w\otimes\overline{u}+\overline{u}\otimes w)\!)\right\|}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{r}_{x}}}_{\eqcolon L_{3}}.

(Estimate of
L_{1}
)

By (
B.3
) or boundedness of Riesz transform, we have:

L_{1}\lesssim{\left\|w\right\|}_{L^{1}_{t}W^{2\theta-1,r}_{x}}.

Note that we have estimated
w
in
L^{s}_{t}W^{\gamma,q}_{x}
. Therefore, given (
5.7
) and (
5.8
), by the above Propositions we can conclude that:

L_{1}\leq C_{u}\lambda^{-\eta}.

(Estimate of
L_{2}
)

Since
w^{(p)}+w^{(c)}=\sigma^{-1}\operatorname{div}\sum_{k}a_{k}(t,x){}_{k}(\sigma x)
, which we saw above, we have:

\partial_{t}(w^{(p)}+w^{(c)})=\sigma^{-1}\cdot\!\sum_{k}\operatorname{div}(\partial_{t}a_{k}\Omegaup(\sigma\cdot)\!),

and hence:

L_{2}\leq{\left\|\mathcal{R}\partial_{t}(w^{(p)}+w^{(c)})\right\|}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{r}_{x}}\lesssim\sigma^{-1}\cdot\!\sum_{k}{\left\|\mathcal{R}\operatorname{div}(\partial_{t}a_{k}{}_{k}(\sigma\cdot)\!)\right\|}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{r}_{x}}.

Since
\mathcal{R}\operatorname{div}
is a Calderón-Zygmund operator on
\mathbb{T}^{d}
, we have:

L_{2}\lesssim\sigma^{-1}\cdot\!\sum_{k}{\left\|\partial_{t}a_{k}\right\|}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}{\left\|{}_{k}\right\|}_{L^{r}}.

Appealing to
(??)
and estimates of
k
listed in
(??)
, we have:

L_{2}\leq C_{u}\nu\kappa^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\sigma^{-1}\mu^{-1+\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle r$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle r$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$r$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$r$}}$}}}.

The desired bound then follows from (
5.9
).

(Estimate of
L_{3}
)

For the last term, we simply use the
L^{r}
boundedness of
\mathcal{R}
, a crude bound, and the estimates obtained above, in conjunction to (
5.10
), to obtain:

L_{3}\lesssim{\left\|w\otimes\overline{u}\right\|}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{r}_{x}}\lesssim{\left\|w\right\|}_{L^{p}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}{\left\|\overline{u}\right\|}_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim_{u}\lambda^{-\eta}.

From these three estimates we can conclude that, for all sufficiently large
\lambda
, there holds:

{\left\|R_{lin}\right\|}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{r}_{x}}\lesssim\lambda^{-\eta}\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 4$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\delta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 4$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}.

(
Correction error
)

Lemma 5.2
(name=Estimates on the correction error,label=thm:lemma:EstRcorr).

For sufficiently large
\lambda
:

{\left\|R_{cor}\right\|}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{r}_{x}}\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 4$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\delta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 4$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}.

Proof.

By boundedness of
\mathcal{R}\operatorname{div}
in
L^{r}
and Hölder’s inequality:

\displaystyle{\left\|R_{cor}\right\|}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{r}_{x}}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle{\left\|(w^{(c)}+w^{(t)})\otimes w\right\|}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{r}_{x}}+{\left\|w^{(p)}\otimes(w^{(c)}+w^{(t)})\right\|}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{r}_{x}}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle{}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle{\left({\left\|w^{(c)}\right\|}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2-r$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2r$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2-r$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2-r$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2-r$}}$}}}_{x}}+{\left\|w^{(t)}\right\|}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\right)}{\left\|w\right\|}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle{}+{\left\|w^{(p)}\right\|}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}{\left({\left\|w^{(c)}\right\|}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2-r$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2r$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2-r$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2-r$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2-r$}}$}}}_{x}}+{\left\|w^{(t)}\right\|}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\right)}.

Note now that, for
r\!\in\![1,2)
,
\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2-r$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2r$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2-r$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2-r$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2-r$}}$}}\geq 2
, and thus
L^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2-r$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2r$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2-r$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2-r$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2-r$}}$}}}\hookrightarrow L^{2}
. Using the propositions above, we can conclude, once again requiring
\nu^{-1}\lesssim\lambda^{-\eta}
for the temporal corrector:

\displaystyle{\left\|w\right\|}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle{\left\|w^{(p)}\right\|}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}+{\left\|w^{(c)}\right\|}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}+{\left\|w^{(t)}\right\|}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim{\left\|\overline{R}\right\|}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}_{x}}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}},

\displaystyle{\left\|w^{(c)}\right\|}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2-r$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2r$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2-r$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2-r$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2r$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2-r$}}$}}}_{x}}+{\left\|w^{(t)}\right\|}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq{}

\displaystyle C_{u}\lambda^{-\eta}.

This completes the proof.
\Diamondx

(Oscillation error)

The following is our counterpart to
[
3 (C-L)
, Lemma 5.8]
.

Lemma 5.3
(name=Estimates on the oscillation error,label=thm:lemma:EstRosc).

Assume that:

\displaystyle\sigma^{-1}\mu^{d-1-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle r$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle r$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$r$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$r$}}$}}}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\lambda^{-\eta}

(5.11)

\displaystyle\sigma^{-1}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\lambda^{-\eta}

(5.12)

\displaystyle\mu^{d-1-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle r$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle r$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$r$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$r$}}$}}}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\lambda^{-\eta}.

(5.13)

Then, for sufficiently large
\lambda
,

{\left\|R_{osc}\right\|}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{r}_{x}}\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 4$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\delta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 4$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}.

Proof.

We will use the decomposition from
(??)
:

R_{osc}=R_{osc,x}+R_{osc,t}+R_{far}.

(Estimate of
R_{osc,x}
)

As is shown in
[
3 (C-L)
]
, the following estimate holds:

{\left\|R_{osc,x}\right\|}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{r}_{x}}\leq C_{u}\sigma^{-1}\mu^{d-1-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle r$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle r$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$r$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$r$}}$}}}.

(Estimate of
R_{osc,t}
)

Using the bound on
g_{\kappa}
, we infer:

{\left\|R_{osc,t}\right\|}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{r}_{x}}={\left\|\sigma^{-1}h_{\kappa}(\sigma t)\operatorname{div}\partial_{t}\overline{R}\right\|}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{r}_{x}}\lesssim\sigma^{-1}{\left\|h_{\kappa}(\sigma\cdot)\right\|}_{L^{1}}C_{u}\leq C_{u}\sigma^{-1}.

(Estimate of
R_{far}
)

We can use
(??)
and
(??)
to obtain:

\displaystyle{\left\|R_{far}\right\|}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{r}_{x}}={}

\displaystyle{\left\|\sum_{k\neq k^{\prime}}a_{k}a_{k^{\prime}}W_{k}(\sigma\cdot)\otimes W_{k^{\prime}}(\sigma\cdot)\right\|}_{L^{1}_{t}L^{r}_{x}}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle{}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\sum_{k\neq k^{\prime}}{\left\|a_{k}\right\|}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}{\left\|a_{k^{\prime}}\right\|}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}{\left\|W_{k}\otimes W_{k^{\prime}}\right\|}_{L^{r}}\leq{}

\displaystyle{}\leq{}

\displaystyle C_{u}\mu^{d-1-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle r$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle r$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$r$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$r$}}$}}}.

The desired estimate thus follows from (
5.11
)-(
5.13
), completing the proof.
\Diamondx

We now eliminate a couple of redundancies.

•

Firstly, if we multiply the LHS of (
5.6
) by
\sigma\mu>1
, we get (
5.3
). (
5.6
) is therefore a consequence of (
5.3
), and may be neglected.

•

Similarly, if we multiply the LHS of (
5.5
) by
\mu>1
, we obtain (
5.11
), which is therefore strictly stronger. We can neglect (
5.5
).

•

Continuing, if we multiply the LHS of (
5.4
) by
\sigma\mu>1
, we obtain (
5.2
). Thus, we neglect (
5.4
);

•

Finally, if we multiply the LHS of (
5.12
) by
\mu^{d-1-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle r$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle r$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$r$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$r$}}$}}}>1
(recall that
r>1
), we obtain the LHS of (
5.11
), meaning (
5.12
) can be neglected.

Since
\eta
is supposed to be small, and
r
is supposed to be close to 1, we can just substitute
r=1,\eta=0
: all other relations are strict inequalities, which will leave room for an
\eta\ll 1
and a
r\sim 1
to be found so that the original system is satisfied. This means that:

•

(
5.10
) become obvious since
\alpha,\beta,\delta,\zeta>0
;

•

r\leq q
reduces to
q>1
;

•

(
5.11
) reduces to
-\delta<0
when we replace
r=1,\eta=0
, so we can remove it;

•

Analogously, (
5.13
) reduces to
-\alpha<0
, which is obvious when
\alpha>0
.

We then substitute
(\beta^{\prime},\delta^{\prime},\zeta^{\prime})\coloneq\alpha^{-1}(\beta,\delta,\zeta)
and eliminate the parameter
\alpha>0
. We also note that (
5.2
) immediately prevents
p
from going above 2, so that we can divide both sides by
\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2-p$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2p$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2-p$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2p$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2-p$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2p$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2-p$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2p$}}$}}>0
. With simple algebra on top of all this, we can rewrite the system as the system found in
(??)
, thus completing the theorem’s proof.

\displaystyle sK_{\delta^{\prime},\gamma,\zeta^{\prime},d,q}\coloneq s{\left(\delta^{\prime}\gamma+\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\zeta^{\prime}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\zeta^{\prime}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\zeta^{\prime}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\zeta^{\prime}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}+\gamma+\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle q$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle d-1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle q$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$q$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$d-1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$q$}}$}}\right)}<{}
